## Reasoning, the last 10
### 2026-11-23/000419-3749w-coder-thinking.md
I'm mapping the Slot16 layout onto the Rsc16 struct: unk00 comes from Slot16's unk04, the unk04 through unk07 bytes from unk08, and unk08 through unk0B from unk0C. Rsc16.unk0C points at the next element's unk00. I'm retyping D_80076248 as D_80076244 plus 4 and accessing the array through the extern alias.

The byte-level layout at +8 through +B of Slot16 is a problem, since that region is a single s32 unk08 and the Rsc16 fields need byte access, which points toward a union. That makes this mapping messy, so I'm setting Rsc16 aside for the hand-written chunk and may leave it pending for now. I'll stop exploring here and wait for the notification.

### 2026-11-23/000421-3750w-coder-thinking.md
It's running very slowly, maybe because a few keys take a long time, especially on large parents like player and RenderWork. I'll end my turn here and wait for the notification.

### 2026-11-23/002420-3751w-coder-thinking.md
Base ref=1 is expected, and the Unkstruct clean result checks out. That leaves 39 clean and 8 refused, which I'll list by name. The file count of 2316 is high because headers are shared across overlays, so I'm wondering whether the engine edits shared bodies that get included in many translation units.

Now I'm sorting the clean keys under their parent entries so they can be folded into batch lines. Some of these keys refuse each other when they sit on the same line, so I'll need to split those up.

Next I'm going to commit the setup ledger rows before running the fold batch, since the tree has to be clean for --fold-batch --write to work. I'll write the 14 lines out as a batch file and start the run in the background.

I'm going to commit the setup edit to the dispositions config first, keeping all 54 absorb rows as pending, since the dispositions check already passed and a ledger-only commit is what the earlier run did.

39 keys passed the dry run, and 8 were refused and are going to the hand chunk. I'm committing the ledger setup, then running the engine with one line per parent.

### 2026-11-23/002425-3752w-coder-thinking.md
The commit step staged the progress log for T21 as well, which is fine since there's precedent for it. Next I'm putting together the batch file.

### 2026-11-23/002432-3753w-coder-thinking.md
I'm leaving the tree alone while things run, and using the wait to plan hand-folds for the refused cases. Next I'll look into why those refusals happened.

### 2026-11-23/002436-3754w-coder-thinking.md
I'm working out the order of the files: the list puts Base first, but the first output line is the Node entry, so I can't yet tell which line belongs to which file. I'll set that aside and just print each line with its filename.

### 2026-11-23/002441-3755w-coder-thinking.md
I'm going through the hand chunk item by item: the base fold, the P10 variant union, the S_8014A3E0 respell, the Node_80167540 conflict, and the small SubA union. Each of them gets handled by hand, and the A098Ctx/func_8003A234 entry is still to come.

I'm now checking the sequencer's union and the pointer arithmetic on arg0 for St_md_MAIN_029, since its size matters there. Next I'll count the use sites for each item that needs a hand fold.

### 2026-11-23/002449-3756w-coder-thinking.md
I'm guessing the 144 "Base" hits are mostly just comments, so the real count is about 800. The 2180 figure for S_8014A3E0 is probably from shared headers being included in many translation units, so I'll check whether those externs are declared per file. I'll wait for the batch and end my turn.
